| 260727-s8g | Patch #157 — activeSet id-shape purge on deactivate. Ashley UAT'd Phase 15/#149 aftermath and reported: ambient fleet-row tap (e.g. "shrok" on thenasty, hostId=3) promotes the row to Tier 1 with `.active-set` glow (expected); tapping the row's DeactivateAction (X-in-circle) should send it back to its Tier 3 host group as ambient but instead leaves it stuck in Tier 1 with the glow still on — visually indistinguishable from a still-active row. Confirmed via live-DOM diag under `~/.claude/identities/tina/bounties/skynet-transformation/deactivate-diag.js`. Root cause: id-shape mismatch in `state.activeSet`. Sequence: (1) ambient fleet-row tap → `addToActiveSet("fleet::HOSTID::SESSIONNAME")` via `fleetRowId()` at `conversation-store.ts:279`; (2) AppShell opens an openTab with a different id shape (`tab-xxx`); Panel's `useEffect([selectedId])` at ~L197 adds `tab-xxx` to activeSet when selectedId flips; (3) activeSet now holds BOTH ids — Tier 1 renders the openTab (fleet-synthetic suppressed by openTabs-wins dedup at store L316), looks fine; (4) `handleRowDeactivate` removed only `row.id` (= `tab-xxx`) and closed the tab; (5) next `computeSnapshot` un-suppresses the fleet-synthetic entry (openTab is gone) and Tier 1 re-promotes it because `state.activeSet.has("fleet::HOSTID::SESSIONNAME")` is still true. Same class of bug as the queued #149 followup-1 pin-nuke (id-shape mismatch scoped to `pinnedIds`), separately queued. **Fix**: exported the existing `fleetRowId` helper as a single source of truth (comment above it at L275-278 already anticipated external callers); Panel's `handleRowDeactivate` now purges BOTH id shapes atomically — `removeFromActiveSet(row.id)` FIRST, then IF (`row.host && row.targetTmuxSession`) `removeFromActiveSet(fleetRowId(parseInt(row.host.id, 10), row.targetTmuxSession))`, then `onDeactivateRow(row)`. Guard skips fleet-id purge for rows without host/session so we never construct a bogus `fleet::null::` string. `removeFromActiveSet` is idempotent so the second call is a safe no-op when only one id shape is present. `parseInt(host.id, 10)` bridges the string→number seam (Host.id: string; fleetRowId(hostId: number)) — matches the store's own bridge at computeSnapshot L296 (`dedupKey(String(parseInt(tab.host.id)), tmux)`). Panel comment block extended with a quick-260727-s8g paragraph documenting the id-shape-mismatch class of bug and referencing the queued #149 followup-1 pin variant. Test 20G (row without targetTmuxSession): fixture `host=valid, targetTmuxSession:null` → click Deactivate → `removeFromActiveSetSpy` called EXACTLY ONCE with `"active-2"`, no call contains `"fleet::"`, no crash. Test 20E untouched. | 260727-r09 | Patch #156 — Gate patches #143 v2 (Terminal) and #148 (PrettyView) visibilitychange handlers on iOS-PWA detection. Full root-cause diagnosis done live with Ashley using DevTools-console diag snippet served on tailnet 8899 (`~/.claude/identities/tina/bounties/chrome-tab-switch-away-forces-manual-reconnect/tab-diag.js`, two iterations to capture message-type strings). Ashley reported Chrome-tab-switch away+back leaves some sessions showing manual "Reconnect / Close" overlay instead of staying connected. First trace (v1 diag) proved close code=1005 wasClean=true (client-side `ws.close()`, not server termination). Second trace (v2 diag) captured message sequence: on visibility→visible, WS #1 receives `sessionCreated → sessionAttached → connected("Session reattached") → disconnected("Connection closed")` in the same millisecond, causing `wasDisconnectedBySSH=true` and the manual overlay. Root chain: patch #143 v2 fires `connectToHost` unconditionally on visibility→visible (was added for iOS PWA where WSes silently die + `readyState` lies). On Chrome desktop, WSes actually stay alive, so force-reconnect closes the OLD WS and opens a NEW one; NEW attaches via `sessionManager.attachWs` (takes over via `sessionTakenOver`), OLD's `ws.on("close")` handler at `terminal.ts:259` then destroys the session via `destroySession` (either directly if isConnected=false, or via `detachWs` timeout), which calls `session.sshConn.end()` — the SSH connection is SHARED (reused at `terminal.ts:403` for the NEW WS), so its `close` event fires on the NEW WS's closure too, which sends `{type:"disconnected",message:"Connection closed"}` at `terminal.ts:2496`. Ashley's insight ("no way this is a Chrome thing") reframed the fix — hiding a tab should be inert, the visibility handler is over-reaching to a class of platforms where its behavior is actively harmful. Fix: keep current force-reconnect on iOS PWA, no-op the handler everywhere else via `isIosPwa()` check (`window.navigator.standalone === true && /iP(hone\|ad\|od)/.test(navigator.userAgent)`). Frontend-only. **Files**: NEW `src/ui/lib/is-ios-pwa.ts` (isIosPwa + SSR guard) + NEW `src/ui/lib/is-ios-pwa.test.ts` (7 tests: 4 combinations of standalone×UA + SSR guard + type-cast round-trip). | 260727-lbr | Aside dismiss clears Claude Code's /btw overlay history before closing it — root cause of Ashley's aside-answer-pollution complaint: aside history within Claude Code's own /btw overlay (not Skynet's state) carried across BTW invocations in the same session, so the model self-referenced prior "please explain" turns and answered as if being asked to re-explain something it had already explained. Fix: change the tmux keystroke sequence sent by the aside-dismiss WS handler from just `Escape` (which closes the overlay but leaves its history intact) to `x` (clears the /btw overlay's in-session history) → 100ms gap → `Escape` (closes overlay). Frontend dismiss path 100% unchanged — the X (Resume) button in ComposeBox.tsx:1506 still fires handleAsideDismiss → WS `{type:'aside_dismissed'}` → backend handler; only the backend's tmux keystroke sequence changed. Change is contained in three code touches: (1) new named exported constant `BTW_CLEAR_HISTORY_KEY = "x"` at claude-session-server.ts near BTW_PROMPT/ASIDE_END_MARKER — lowercase `x` per Ashley's "X button" phrasing (single-key like `f` for fork / `Esc` for close per the ASIDE_END_MARKER footer pattern); if UAT reveals a different key (e.g. `c`, `Ctrl+L`), the constant is the one-line fix so a wrong-key UAT doesn't require a full replan; (2) `sendEscapeToBtw` renamed to `dismissBtw` at claude-session-server.ts:194 — new shape uses TWO `execCommand` calls separated by `await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 100))`, mirroring patch #152's `injectBtw` two-call pattern (a batched key burst risks being absorbed as a paste by Claude Code's Ink REPL, so the 100ms gap guarantees the clear-history keystroke and the Escape keystroke arrive as distinct events); 100ms chosen (not 200ms like injectBtw) because dismiss is two single-key presses vs. injectBtw's ~300-char paste — no paste-detection risk on the shorter keys, so the shortest known-good gap suffices; rationale recorded in the constant's docstring so future refactors don't blindly copy 200ms; (3) the ONE production caller at claude-session-server.ts (former line 1705, inside the `aside_dismissed` WS handler) switched from `sendEscapeToBtw(sshConn, currentTmuxSession)` to `dismissBtw(sshConn, currentTmuxSession)`. Test side: 5 new unit tests locking the two-call ordered contract (BTW_CLEAR_HISTORY_KEY sent first, `Escape` sent second, ~100ms gap between with fake timers, log-and-swallow on either-call failure); existing integration test at claude-session-server.aside.integration.test.ts updated to assert the mock execCommand receives BOTH keystrokes in order (not just Escape); PrettyView.tsx doc-comments at L231/305/633 sweep-updated `sendEscapeToBtw → dismissBtw` so `grep -rn "sendEscapeToBtw" src/` is a zero-match tripwire (planner's discretion — cheap insurance against name drift). New `__dismissBtwForTests` test seam mirrors the existing `__injectBtwForTests` convention. Verification all green: `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0 at both the mid-task gate and after Task 2; `npx vitest run` → **653/653 across 50 test files** (baseline 648 + exactly 5 new = 4 dismissBtw shape tests + 1 clear-history-key constant assertion; integration test count unchanged — assertion added inside existing test). All grep checkpoints pass: `BTW_CLEAR_HISTORY_KEY` present in server + tests; `dismissBtw` present in server + WS handler; `sendEscapeToBtw` 0 matches in src/ (tripwire holds). WS frame shape `{type:'aside_dismissed', hostId, tmuxSession}` and frontend `handleAsideDismiss` UNTOUCHED (per plan non-negotiable — only the tmux keystroke sequence changed). Two atomic commits on `feat/tab-title-from-tmux`: `60c1ded` (feat: dismissBtw two-keystroke shape) + `5adad8a` (test: integration + PrettyView doc-comment sweep). Zero other files touched. NO Co-Authored-By trailer (fork convention). No push. No deploy this quick — batched pending Ashley greenlight per "every build → deploy is a new may-I" rule from patch #35, though this is trigger 3 territory (aside-answer-pollution is actively degrading her live use every time she stacks asides in a session) so SOLO-DEPLOY carveout is available on her nod. `~/.claude/identities/tina/skynet-patches.md` catalog write-up deferred to deploy time per batch-writeups-until-deploy rule (catalog is ~19-22 patches deep now including this pair). | 260727-kbw | Phase 15 UAT followup — fleet-pin load-order race fix. Ashley UAT'd Phase 15 (server-side pinned-conversation persistence, shipped 14:20 UTC) with "pins are not working, they last only until I close the app." Tina verified server side was working (curl round-trip via `/user-preferences` GET+PUT confirmed her `fleet::7::aqua` pin IS persisted; deployed AppShell chunk has correct mount-effect wiring: `getPinnedIds()` [minified `Qe`] then `hydratePinnedIdsFromServer` [minified `Ct`]). Root cause: race in `updateOpenTabs` pruner at `src/ui/state/conversation-store.ts:536-547`. On page load: `state.pinnedIds`, `state.fleetSessions` both empty (fleet loads independently async). Panel mount effect fires `getPinnedIds()` microtask → resolves `["fleet::7::aqua"]` → `hydratePinnedIdsFromServer` sets `pinnedIds = {fleet::7::aqua}`. Any subsequent `updateOpenTabs` re-emit (routine WS tab-list update) triggers pruner (now `pinnedIds.size > 0`). If `state.fleetSessions` still hasn't loaded yet, `fleetPinKeepSet` is empty → fleet pin isn't in `nextIds` (never in openTabs — it's fleet), isn't in (empty) `fleetPinKeepSet` → PRUNED. Pin disappears from UI; server still has it; next refresh rehydrates; race repeats. Ashley confirmed the caveat about later empty re-emits doesn't apply — fleet list is loaded ONCE per page and stays that reference for the rest of the session. **Fix (Ashley-designed):** defer the panel's `getPinnedIds()` + `hydratePinnedIdsFromServer()` hydration until fleet sessions have loaded at least once. When fleet has loaded, `fleetPinKeepSet` in any subsequent `updateOpenTabs` will include the fleet pin, and the pruner won't nuke it. **Slice 1** (`e6aede4`): store — added `fleetSessionsLoaded: boolean` to `ConversationState` (default false); `updateFleetSessions()` unconditionally sets `fleetSessionsLoaded: true` on state update (empty array counts as "loaded" — user may genuinely have zero fleet sessions), preserving the existing same-sessions no-op short-circuit for the array itself but forcing `notify()` on false→true flag flip; new `getFleetSessionsLoadedSnapshot()` + exported `useFleetSessionsLoaded(): boolean` via `useSyncExternalStore` mirroring `usePinnedIds()` pattern; new test helper `__resetFleetSessionsForTest()` mirroring existing `__resetPinnedIdsForTest`/`__resetActiveSetForTest` pattern for clean test starting points. 7 new store tests: `fleetSessionsLoaded starts false`; non-empty array flips to true; empty array ALSO flips to true (empty-is-loaded); subsequent identical calls don't re-notify solely on flag; `useFleetSessionsLoaded` returns current value and re-renders on flip; **regression test locking the exact bug**: `updateFleetSessions([{hostId:7, sessionName:"aqua"}])` → `hydratePinnedIdsFromServer(["fleet::7::aqua"])` → `updateOpenTabs([])` with empty tab list → assert `fleet::7::aqua` STILL in `pinnedIds` (would have been nuked pre-fix). **Slice 2** (`fd3efbb`): panel — imported `useFleetSessionsLoaded` + added `useRef` import; replaced the mount effect at PrettyConversationsPanel.tsx:204-218 with fleet-loaded-gated version — subscribes to `fleetSessionsLoaded`, adds `hydratedRef = useRef(false)` guard to prevent double-hydration on any spurious flag re-flip (defense-in-depth even though Ashley confirms it stays true after first flip). Effect gates on `!fleetSessionsLoaded → return`, then `hydratedRef.current → return`, then flips ref true and fires the same `getPinnedIds()` → `hydratePinnedIdsFromServer(ids)` async IIFE with cancelled-guard. Preserved existing Phase 15 (a)/(b)/(c) block comment; added a new (d) paragraph explaining the fleet-loaded gate + the race it closes (tagged with quick-task slug `quick-260727-kbw` for future greppability). New Test 22 mirroring Test 21 mock pattern: mount does NOT call `getPinnedIds` when `fleetSessionsLoaded=false`; when flag flips to true, `getPinnedIds` called exactly once + `hydratePinnedIdsFromServer` called exactly once with resolved ids. Verification all green: `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0; `npx vitest run` → **648/648 across 50 test files** (baseline 647 + 1 new panel test; store file grew 55 → 62 tests including 7 new = +7 store, +1 panel, 0 regressions). All grep checkpoints pass: `fleetSessionsLoaded` 8 hits in store, `useFleetSessionsLoaded` export 1 hit, panel gate + `hydratedRef` 4 hits each. Two atomic commits on `feat/tab-title-from-tmux`, NO Co-Authored-By trailer (fork convention). No push, no deploy — Ashley calls the deploy separately per the "every build → deploy is a new may-I" rule from patch #35; this is trigger 3 (actively broken in production Ashley is currently hitting), so SOLO-DEPLOY carveout applies pending her nod. Pre-warn on first-hard-refresh `HTTP2_PROTOCOL_ERROR` (Caddy H2 stream fallout during container recreate — close+reopen tab spawns fresh H2 connection; not a real deploy failure). `~/.claude/identities/tina/skynet-patches.md` catalog write-up deferred per batch-writeups-until-deploy rule (catalog is ~15-18 patches behind now including Phase 15 waves + this fix; all pinned together at deploy-recommendation time). Anti-scope-creep held: no forbidden surface touched. Related bounties: `skynet-transformation` master (Phase 15 UAT followup folds into its timeline per one-project rule — do NOT create sibling bounty). | 2026-07-27 | fd3efbb | [260727-kbw-fleet-pin-load-race](./quick/260727-kbw-fleet-pin-load-race/) |

Root cause: Phase 14's arm-emitter useEffect at `src/ui/features/pretty-view/PrettyView.tsx:909` short-circuited `aside_arm` only when the last user turn's `content.trimStart().startsWith("/id ")` — a check that catches raw-text `/id save` typed into SSH-attached tmux panes (round-trips through JSONL as literal string), but MISSES the harness slash-UI invocation path where `/id tina` lands in JSONL as XML wrappers `<command-message>id</command-message>\n<command-name>/id</command-name>\n<command-args>tina</command-args>` (content starts with `<`, not `/id `). Harness slash-UI is Ashley's PRIMARY /id invocation path from pretty-view, so every /id from that surface armed a phantom aside. Fix: extract module-local const arrow `isIdCommand = (content: string): boolean => content.trimStart().startsWith("/id ") \|\| content.includes("<command-name>/id</command-name>")` above the useEffect + swap the inline check for `if (isIdCommand(lastUserContent)) return;` at the callsite. XML substring `<command-name>/id</command-name>` chosen as the marker because it is not a legal substring of prose — it only appears when the harness itself wraps the command, so false-positive risk is zero. Helper stays module-local (no new shared util file — Phase 14 no-new-deps posture preserved). Test 9 mirrors Test 7 shape with realistic UAT payload (`<command-args>tina</command-args>` sibling) → asserts `aside_arm` NOT called. Tests 1-8 explicitly locked unchanged; Tests 7 (/id-then-real-msg still arms) + 8 (only last user turn matters) remain valid regression guards. TDD gate followed: RED (Test 9 isolated → `expected […aside_arm…] to be undefined`) → GREEN (helper + callsite swap → Test 9 passes) → REFACTOR skipped (helper landed final on first pass) — RED+GREEN combined into single atomic commit matching Phase 14 commit style precedent (`f029e44` also bundles impl+tests). Verification all green: `npm run type-check` clean; `npx vitest run PrettyView.aside.test.tsx` 9/9 (was 8/8 pre-plan); full sweep `npm test` 604/604 across 49 files (was 603/603 — net +1). Grep gates: `isIdCommand` declaration+callsite both present; `<command-name>/id<` XML substring present; Test 9 title present. Frontmatter note (non-blocking): plan `<truths>` said baseline "33 → 34" tests but aside test file's actual baseline was 8 → 9 (33/34 figure appears to have referenced aggregate PrettyView scope) — behavioral truth-set fully satisfied, documented in SUMMARY.md for traceability, no action. Two files, +73/-1 (`src/ui/features/pretty-view/PrettyView.tsx` +17/-1, `src/ui/features/pretty-view/PrettyView.aside.test.tsx` +56/0). Single atomic commit on `feat/tab-title-from-tmux`. | 260724-bwi | Patch #148 — PrettyView WebSocket auto-reconnect. Root cause diagnosed 2026-07-24 with Ashley via #147 SkynetLog telemetry: `PrettyView.tsx:447-457` ws.onclose handler had explicit "Do NOT auto-reopen" comment, just set `errorMessage = "Connection closed"` and gave up. When claude-session WS closed for ANY reason (iOS PWA background, deploy container recreate, network flap), no reopen path existed. Terminal.tsx's ssh WS reconnects fine (#143v2 works, proven by 3 clean end-to-end reconnect cycles in the SkynetLog file with ZERO failure entries), but PrettyView's SEPARATE WebSocket to /claude-session/websocket/ was single-shot. Ashley's confirmed workaround: toggle pretty view off + on via key combo = unmount+remount = useEffect re-runs = fresh WS. Fix: mirror Terminal.tsx's proven retry pattern into PrettyView. Concrete changes: (a) new module-scope `MAX_RECONNECT_ATTEMPTS = 5` const, (b) refs `reconnectAttemptsRef`/`reconnectTimeoutRef`/`paneKeyRef`/`statusRef`, (c) `retryKey` state added to the WS-open useEffect deps so bumping it re-runs the effect with cleanup+fresh WS, (d) `paneKeyRef` distinguishes pane-change (full state reset preserved) from retry re-run (state preserved), (e) `statusRef` mirror avoids stale-status closure issues in onclose handler, (f) replaced onclose "Do NOT auto-reopen" body with retry-scheduling logic gated on `statusRef.current !== 'inactive'` and `reconnectAttemptsRef.current < MAX_RECONNECT_ATTEMPTS` with `Math.min(2000 * attempt, 8000)` backoff (2/4/6/8/8s = ~28s total window), (g) ws.onopen clears `errorMessage(null)` and increments cleared attempts (so retries never show transient empty state — errorMessage stays "Connection closed" through backoff until a NEW WS actually opens), (h) new mount-once visibilitychange useEffect: on `document.hidden` clears any pending timer, on visible resets counter to 0 and bumps retryKey if WS not OPEN and status not inactive (direct fix for Ashley's iOS PWA long-background case where retries might burn through while suspended), (i) cleanup fn clears pending reconnectTimeoutRef before ws.close() so unmount never fires stale retries. "inactive" state semantics preserved throughout — no retry, no reopen, no errorMessage clear when server sent an authoritative terminal frame. 4 new tests (retry-on-close fires new WS after 2s and clears errorMessage on onopen; max-5-attempt cap holds; visibilitychange:visible resets counter and reopens even after cap exhaust; inactive state short-circuits ALL retry paths) + 3 existing Phase 05 tests all pass = 7/7 green. | 260723-9nv | Compose-box textarea auto-grow with contents (patch #135) — replaces the newline-count `rows = Math.min(6, text.split("\n").length)` heuristic at ComposeBox.tsx:517 (which left long single-line messages clipped because wrapped visual lines never add a `\n`) with a `useLayoutEffect` on `[text]` that drives `el.style.height` imperatively off `scrollHeight`, capped at 6 line-heights. Mechanic: `el.style.height='auto'` first (mandatory — without it, scrollHeight only grows, never shrinks on delete), then `Math.min(el.scrollHeight, maxHeightPxRef.current)`, then set `overflowY='auto'` only at the cap so the scrollbar appears only when the 6-row ceiling is hit. `maxHeightPxRef` (new `useRef<number|null>(null)`) is computed once on first pass from `parseFloat(getComputedStyle(el).lineHeight) × 6` with 144px fallback for JSDOM `normal`-keyword branch where parseFloat resolves to NaN. `useLayoutEffect` imported alongside existing React hooks. `<Textarea rows={rows}>` → `<Textarea rows={1}>` (SSR/initial-paint fallback; effect immediately overrides on mount). All other styling untouched: `min-h-8!` (32px CSS floor for empty state via the Tailwind v4 important-suffix cascade beats shadcn's `min-h-[80px]` base), `resize-none`, `bg-[rgba(10,12,20,0.5)]!`, `pr-10` (patch #129 inside-Send padding), border, focus ring, warm-black well, aux buttons, meter well, queueArmed overlay, inside-textarea Send button (position anchored to wrapper, not textarea, so no adjustment needed). Both were rendering simultaneously on mobile-in-conversation per Ashley's diag (fixed at 8,8 z-30 + legacy at 0,0). Identity badge already surfaces conversation identity so the label was dead weight too. Bundled cleanup: removed now-unused `activeConversationLabel` derivation, `Separator` + `Button` imports, and dead `isMobileViewScreen` const (Rule 1 dead-code cleanup, executor deviation documented in SUMMARY). Fix ONLY the color; separate follow-up will reclaim ~5/6 of the vertical space per Ashley's ask (trim compose `py-2` + adjust safe-area padding). Fix (d) `PrettyConversationsPanel.tsx`: added `useEffect(() => { if (selectedId) addToActiveSet(selectedId); }, [selectedId])` right after the hoisted `useActiveSet()` call — root cause of Ashley's "active conversations not lighting up / not glowing like the mock" (diag showed ALL 32 rendered rows had `pv-row pv-row--mobile ambient` class, i.e. NONE in activeSet, despite one being her currently-open session): `addToActiveSet` only fired from `handleRowSelect` (click path), so URL-fragment restore into `#tab=tmux:host:session&active=0` bypassed handleRowSelect entirely, left activeSet empty, and rows stayed `ambient` (flat quiet treatment) instead of the full pretty-view bubble glow. New useEffect catches every selectedId change regardless of path (click, URL restore, tab switch, programmatic) because they all funnel through `selectedId`. `addToActiveSet` idempotent so double-fires from click-that-also-changes-selectedId are harmless. Fix (e) `pretty-conversations.css` + `PrettyConversationsPanel.tsx`: added `.pv-panel-group { display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 8px; }` and applied `pv-panel-group` class to all 3 group wrappers (pinned rows now wrapped in an explicit group div; regular hosts group + RDP-sentinel group converted from bare `flex flex-col`). Root cause of Ashley's "no spacing between list items" was that my patch #142 `.pv-panel-scroll gap:8px` IS applied but only between GROUPS — individual rows within each group's `<div class="flex flex-col">` wrapper were edge-to-edge because the wrappers had no gap. 8px within-group matches 8px between-groups for consistent visual rhythm. Fix (f) `PrettyConversationsPanel.tsx`: removed the mobile-conditional gate on `pv-panel-header` so "Conversations" title renders on mobile too (was suppressed by a `showDesktopTitle`-style gate — Ashley confirmed the prior handoff note "deliberately left off per Phase 10 design" was WRONG and made up in a prior session). | 260724-7ow | SSH terminal auto-reconnect on PWA visibilitychange (patch #143) — fixes iOS PWA backgrounding failure mode where the 8-attempt exponential-backoff reconnect budget (2s→4s→8s cap, ~50s) burns through against a throttled iOS tab while Ashley is on another app, leaving `shouldNotReconnectRef=true` and the manual "Connection lost" overlay stuck even though the target's tmux was up the entire time. New sibling `useEffect` in `Terminal.tsx` placed immediately after the existing lifecycle-reset effect at line 328 (co-located with the reconnect refs it manipulates): (a) on `document.hidden===true`, clears any scheduled `reconnectTimeoutRef` timeout and resets `reconnectAttempts.current=0` so the budget isn't burned against a throttled tab; (b) on `document.hidden===false`, if `ws.readyState !== OPEN` AND `wasDisconnectedBySSH.current===false` AND `isUnmountingRef.current===false`, resets all reconnect state flags (`shouldNotReconnectRef=false`, `isReconnectingRef=false`, `isConnectingRef=false`, `reconnectAttempts=0`, `wasConnectedRef=false`, `wasDisconnectedBySSH=false`), clears `updateConnectionError(null)`, hides overlay via `setShowDisconnectedOverlay(false)`, and calls `connectToHost(terminal.cols, terminal.rows)`. Boundary preserved: `wasDisconnectedBySSH.current===true` (auth fail, target-side ssh session closed, `~.` typed) does NOT auto-reconnect — manual overlay Reconnect stays the affordance. Deliberate divergence from the manual Reconnect handler (Terminal.tsx:3024-3037): NO `terminal.clear()` on the visibility path — tmux repaint on reattach handles restoration, and the clear was the visible-flicker cause in the manual overlay path. Deps: `[terminal, updateConnectionError]` matching file convention (file-wide `eslint-disable react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` at line 1, refs + setState omitted, connectToHost is a stable function declaration). Tests: 1 new structural (Test 6 — regex-pinned baseline of `visibilitychange`/`document.hidden`/`terminal.clear()`/`wasDisconnectedBySSH.current` occurrence counts to catch drift; JSDoc mid-execution rephrased to avoid literal `terminal.clear()` token that would have inflated count from 8→9) + 4 new behavioral in a new `describe('Terminal - patch #143 visibilitychange auto-reconnect', ...)` block using the file's established byte-for-byte helper-mirror pattern (Tests 7/8/9/10 covering hidden-branch cancel+reset, visible-branch full reset+connectToHost fire, target-terminated boundary NO-fire, and WS.OPEN no-op — Test 10 is one of the plan's must_haves.truths so earned its own test). | 260728-r1h | Fix Kiro CLI wrapper defeating claude-session detection — walk pane_pid descendants for `claude` comm. Root cause verified live on thenasty via SSH: AWS Kiro CLI's `kiro-cli-term` pty wrapper sets `argv[0]='bash'` on the shell it launches, so `/proc/<pane_pid>/cmdline` reads `bash (kiro-cli-term)` and tmux's `pane_current_command` returns `bash` even though `/proc/<pane_pid>/comm` is `kiro-cli-term`. Detection at `session-file-discovery.ts:47-51` did strict `trimmedCommand === "claude"` exact-match → returned `not_claude` for every thenasty pane, meanwhile `claude` was running as a grandchild (kiro-cli-term → bash --login → claude). All 5 thenasty identities (beatrice, nelly, shrok, vicky, yolanda) rendered "no active Claude session" fallback in pretty-view. Fix: replaced strict pane_current_command match with a POSIX `ps -eo pid=,ppid=,comm=` + awk fixed-point BFS descendant-tree walk seeded from `pane_pid`. Emits the first PID with `comm='claude'` anywhere in the tree (including `pane_pid` itself as the backcompat direct-claude case). Uses that PID for the existing CWD/JSONL discovery script (unchanged byte-for-byte except `${pid}` → `${claudePid}`). Handles wrappers of arbitrary depth without hardcoding. `queryPaneCurrentCommand` import removed; `pid_unavailable` kept in the type union for backcompat but no longer emitted (folded into `no_tmux_session`). Wire-type unaffected — frontend types `reason` as bare `string` and only switches on `holding_timeout`. Tests: 8 new vitest cases (Kiro wrapper 3-level, deeper 4-level wrapper, direct-claude backcompat, no-claude tree, null pane_pid, walk timeout, CWD reject, CWD empty). Backend typecheck via `npm run build:backend && npm run build` clean (patch #154 lesson honored); 766/766 tests green. Single atomic commit `67f94a4` on `feat/tab-title-from-tmux`.
